## 58. Maintenance Risk, Example and Responsibility Models

The risk register describes concrete failure conditions, operational impacts, target controls, accountable owners and residual direction. The example catalog traces realistic requests and acknowledgements across Inventory, Manufacturing, Quality, Procurement, Safety/HSE and Finance without direct writes.

The RACI assigns exactly one accountable and one different responsible role per activity. A current-versus-target matrix separates existing organization/master-data/platform evidence from planned maintenance aggregates and future predictive/mobile functions.

**Controlled concepts:** risk register; example catalog; RACI; current versus target; effect requests; approval; reconciliation; specific risk; residual direction.

### 58.1 Maintenance risk register

The register states a concrete failure condition, resulting operational consequence, target control owner and remaining risk direction. Residual direction is a design expectation, not a claim that controls operate today.

| Risk ID | Maintenance area | Risk | Current condition | Impact | Target mitigation | Owner | Residual-risk direction |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MR-001 | Technical object | Wrong equipment selected | the request, order or scan resolves to an equipment ID other than the physical tag at the work point. | work and history attach to the wrong technical object and the intended defect remains untreated. | require tag or serial scan at dispatch and technician start; block a mismatch and record a supervisor-approved identity correction. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for wrong equipment selected; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |
| MR-002 | Technical object | Duplicate equipment identity | identity creation accepts a duplicate tenant, manufacturer and serial combination or a second active equipment number. | maintenance history, warranty eligibility and installed configuration split across two records. | enforce scoped uniqueness, route suspected duplicates to stewardship, and merge only through an immutable survivor-and-alias decision. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for duplicate equipment identity; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |
| MR-003 | Technical object | Wrong functional location | planning resolves an equipment item to an obsolete or incompatible functional-location revision. | crew routing, isolation scope, cost assignment and criticality defaults are wrong. | validate the effective-dated installation at order release and snapshot both location ID and hierarchy revision. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for wrong functional location; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |
| MR-004 | Technical object | Asset hierarchy corruption | a parent move creates a cycle, incompatible parentage or overlapping effective dates. | as-maintained configuration cannot be replayed and affected work populations become unreliable. | apply acyclic and compatibility invariants, prohibit interval overlap, and replay all affected descendants before publishing the revision. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for asset hierarchy corruption; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |
| MR-005 | Technical object | Equipment moved without history | the current location pointer is overwritten without an installation or removal event. | past work appears to have occurred at the new location and custody cannot be defended. | record effective-dated remove and install events with actor, reason and source order; never update current placement without history. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for equipment moved without history; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |
| MR-006 | Technical object | Component swap not recorded | a serialized component is fitted or removed without a position-specific event or with an overlapping installation interval. | the failed component, warranty unit and reliability population cannot be identified. | require paired removal and installation events, unique active position occupancy, and Inventory serial-custody acknowledgement. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for component swap not recorded; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |
| MR-007 | Criticality and strategy | Wrong criticality class | criticality inputs or the approved score are changed without a new effective-dated assessment. | priority, PM tolerance, spare policy and escalation no longer reflect approved consequence. | version factor values, calculation method, evidence and approval; recalculate dependent decisions prospectively. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for wrong criticality class passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-008 | Criticality and strategy | Maintenance strategy wrong | the selected preventive, predictive or run-to-failure strategy does not match failure consequence and operating context. | a preventable functional failure is accepted or unnecessary work is imposed. | require a consequence review, applicability statement and Reliability approval before strategy publication. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for maintenance strategy wrong passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-009 | Preventive maintenance | PM interval wrong | a calendar or usage interval is configured with the wrong unit, basis or tolerance for the approved strategy. | work is called too early or after the controlled exposure limit. | validate interval, unit, offset and tolerance against the approved strategy revision before plan activation. | Maintenance Planner | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for pm interval wrong; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-010 | Preventive maintenance | PM due date wrong | the due calculation uses an incorrect start date, factory calendar, counter baseline or time zone. | compliance aging and scheduling priority are materially misstated. | persist calculation inputs and formula version; independently recalculate sampled calls and expose due-date corrections. | Maintenance Planner | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for pm due date wrong; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-011 | Preventive maintenance | PM deferred without approval | a due call is moved beyond tolerance without named approver, expiry, compensating control and retained original due date. | known exposure is hidden from overdue reporting and operating decisions. | use a separate deferral decision with authority, reason, exposure, expiry and immutable original due date. | Maintenance Planner | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for pm deferred without approval; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-012 | Preventive maintenance | PM skipped silently | a generated call disappears or is closed without a skip decision and justification. | mandatory or risk-based work is absent from compliance history. | prohibit deletion, require an approved skip event, and keep skipped calls visible in compliance measures. | Maintenance Planner | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for pm skipped silently; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-013 | Intake and priority | Maintenance request lost | an accepted intake event has no durable request ID, queue entry or owner acknowledgement. | reported defects receive no assessment and the reporter cannot trace disposition. | persist intake before acknowledgement, monitor untriaged age, and escalate requests without an accountable queue owner. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for maintenance request lost; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-014 | Intake and priority | Duplicate notification | two notifications represent the same equipment, symptom and event but are planned independently. | backlog, failure counts and work orders are duplicated. | show candidate matches, require a merge decision with a surviving notification, and preserve all source reporters. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for duplicate notification; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-015 | Intake and priority | Priority understated | triage omits safety, environmental, quality, production or criticality impact and assigns a lower priority. | response is delayed beyond the consequence tolerance. | calculate a transparent baseline from all impact dimensions and require reasoned approval for downward override. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for priority understated; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-016 | Intake and priority | Priority overstated | an emergency label is applied without corresponding consequence or response criterion. | planned critical work is displaced and emergency controls become routine. | require declared impact and expiry for override; review emergency use and restore displaced backlog explicitly. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for priority overstated; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-017 | Intake and priority | Breakdown not declared | functional failure and unavailable time are recorded as ordinary corrective work. | breakdown response, downtime and failure statistics are understated. | require breakdown flag and malfunction start when function is lost; reconcile to Manufacturing stop events. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for breakdown not declared passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-018 | Intake and priority | Breakdown end falsified | the malfunction end is entered before function was actually restored or verified. | MTTR and availability are overstated and production-loss chronology diverges. | derive end from verified functional restoration, retain late corrections, and reconcile to release and Manufacturing restart. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for breakdown end falsified passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-019 | Work management | Work order created for wrong asset | order creation copies the wrong equipment or installed-component identity from intake. | technicians isolate and repair the wrong object while the defect remains. | compare request, notification, physical tag and current installation; require correction before release. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for work order created for wrong asset; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-020 | Work management | Work order released without approval | status reaches Released without an authorized approver for the risk, cost and scope. | labor, permits and stock can be committed without accountable authorization. | enforce state-transition authority, approval revision matching and negative tests for self-approval. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for work order released without approval; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-021 | Work management | Work order starts without permit | technician start is accepted before the required permit and isolation are valid for the equipment and time. | hazardous work begins outside Safety authority. | query permit and isolation acknowledgement at start, block expired or mismatched scope, and record the checked revision. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after field sampling demonstrates the work order starts without permit control; deliberate physical bypass remains possible. |
| MR-022 | Work management | Work order starts without material | execution starts though required reserved or issued spares are unavailable and no approved workaround exists. | equipment is dismantled and remains unavailable while waiting for parts. | include critical material readiness in release/start gates and require planner-approved scope change for alternatives. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for work order starts without material; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-023 | Work management | Technician unqualified | assignment or start ignores required skill, certification, equipment class or validity date. | work quality, safety and certification evidence are invalid. | match operation requirements to current personal qualifications and block start after expiry or scope mismatch. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for technician unqualified passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-024 | Work management | Contractor unqualified | a supplier worker is dispatched without accepted competence, sponsorship or site authorization. | external work proceeds without verified capability or accountable access. | require named worker qualification, contract scope, sponsor and access validity before assignment acknowledgement. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after field sampling demonstrates the contractor unqualified control; deliberate physical bypass remains possible. |
| MR-025 | Work management | Shared terminal attribution loss | multiple technicians submit work under a shared session or a correction replaces the original actor. | individual labor, measurements and signatures cannot be defended. | require step-up personal authentication for confirmations and preserve original actor plus correcting approver. | Maintenance Manager | Low after denied-command and object-scope tests cover shared terminal attribution loss; privileged misuse remains monitored. |
| MR-026 | Work management | Labor time falsified | labor start, stop or duration is entered without credible assignment, chronology or supervisor evidence. | maintenance cost and productivity measures are misstated. | validate overlap and order state, capture source and corrections, and require supervisor review for exceptions. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for labor time falsified passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-027 | Downtime and failure | Downtime misclassified | equipment active, maintenance active, waiting and Manufacturing production-loss intervals are collapsed into one duration. | MTTR, wrench time and production loss drive the wrong decisions. | maintain separate interval types with independent owners and reconcile boundary timestamps. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for downtime misclassified passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-028 | Spares and tools | Spare shortage | required quantity is not available by the planned need time and the shortage is not acknowledged. | repair start or restoration is delayed without visible exposure. | send a versioned demand to Inventory, record confirmed quantity/date or shortage, and escalate critical uncovered demand. | Inventory | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for spare shortage; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |

| MR-111 | Intake and priority | Safety concern stripped during triage | conversion to a notification or order omits the reporter safety flag, hazard text or attachment. | planning can proceed without required hazard review or permit controls. | carry safety fields immutably into downstream records and require Safety acknowledgement before removing a flag. | Safety/HSE | Medium-low after field sampling demonstrates the safety concern stripped during triage control; deliberate physical bypass remains possible. |
| MR-112 | Intake and priority | Emergency label used to bypass planning | emergency classification allows work to start without minimum scope, competence, isolation and material checks. | urgent response introduces uncontrolled technical and safety exposure. | define irreducible emergency prerequisites and require retrospective scope review without converting containment into release. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for emergency label used to bypass planning; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-113 | Intake and priority | Breakdown start backdated | malfunction start is changed to an earlier time without source evidence and correction approval. | downtime, response performance and production loss are manipulated. | retain reported and corrected timestamps, require evidence and approval, and restate dependent measures visibly. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for breakdown start backdated passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-114 | Intake and priority | Production impact not communicated | Maintenance changes expected outage, readiness or release timing without sending a versioned request to Manufacturing. | production schedules and WIP decisions rely on stale capacity assumptions. | publish scoped outage and readiness changes with revision, causation and acknowledgement; age unacknowledged requests. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for production impact not communicated passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-115 | Intake and priority | Quality impact not communicated | a failure, repair or calibration event with potential product impact is closed without a Quality assessment request. | affected inspections or product lots may remain accepted without review. | derive impact triggers, send equipment/time/lot scope to Quality, and block final closure until acknowledgement where policy requires. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for quality impact not communicated passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-116 | Intake and priority | Notification converted to wrong order | notification conversion links to an order for another equipment item, symptom or revision. | execution does not address the assessed defect and genealogy becomes misleading. | validate equipment and scope equivalence and preserve an immutable notification-to-order causation link. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for notification converted to wrong order; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-117 | Intake and priority | Anonymous request defeats attribution | a request affecting safety or urgent maintenance lacks authenticated reporter or sponsored anonymous channel. | clarification, accountability and misuse investigation are impaired. | require authenticated identity or a controlled public channel with location, contact and intake-agent sponsorship. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for anonymous request defeats attribution; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-118 | Work management | Work order uses obsolete revision | a released order continues with a task-list or order revision superseded by an approved safety or engineering change. | field execution follows instructions known to be out of date. | identify affected released orders, suspend unsafe execution and require impact review plus approval before adopting the new revision. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for work order uses obsolete revision; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-119 | Work management | Completion evidence fabricated | required readings, photos, signatures or test results are copied, reused or submitted without source provenance. | an incomplete repair can appear verified and support unsafe release. | bind evidence hashes to operation, equipment, actor and capture time; sample anomalies and block unverifiable mandatory evidence. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for completion evidence fabricated passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-120 | Work management | Operation sequence bypassed | a technician confirms a dependent operation before its prerequisite, inspection or isolation step. | assembly can be closed with an unverified interface or missed safety checkpoint. | evaluate the operation dependency graph on every confirmation and require a separately approved order revision for any changed sequence. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for operation sequence bypassed passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-121 | Work management | Required measurement omitted | technical completion is attempted without the measurement, unit, limit and instrument required by the operation. | acceptance criteria cannot be demonstrated. | make required readings structured completion guards and validate unit, limit and instrument status. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for required measurement omitted passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-122 | Work management | Cancelled order hides consumed resources | an order with labor, issued material or external service is cancelled without dispositioning those actuals. | Inventory custody and Finance cost reconciliation remain open. | prohibit terminal cancellation until actuals are returned, transferred or accepted and all domain acknowledgements reconcile. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for cancelled order hides consumed resources; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-123 | Work management | Dispatch acknowledged by wrong technician | assignment acknowledgement comes from a different user, device or crew than the dispatch recipient. | work ownership and competence checks apply to the wrong person. | bind acknowledgement to named assignee and device session; require explicit supervisor reassignment. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for dispatch acknowledged by wrong technician passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-124 | Work management | Schedule freeze bypassed | work inside the frozen horizon is inserted, moved or removed without change authority. | crew, material, permit and production commitments become unreliable. | version the frozen schedule and require reason, impact assessment and approver for every change. | Maintenance Manager | Low after lifecycle, idempotency and correction tests for schedule freeze bypassed; authorized human exception remains visible and reviewable. |
| MR-125 | Work management | Emergency work displaces critical backlog silently | urgent work consumes protected capacity without recording which committed jobs were displaced. | critical overdue exposure is hidden and never rescheduled. | record displacement links, recalculate exposure, and require scheduler disposition of every affected job. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for emergency work displaces critical backlog silently passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-126 | Work management | Paused work remains recorded as active | a technician waits for material, permit, vendor or access while the operation and labor timers remain running. | active-repair duration and personal labor inflate while the true dependency delay is hidden. | close active labor at pause, select a typed waiting state and resume only through a new attributed execution interval. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for paused work remains recorded as active passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-127 | Work management | Rework scope added without reapproval | failed work adds operations, parts or hazards to the released revision without approval. | the crew executes outside authorized cost and safety scope. | create a new order revision, reassess prerequisites and obtain approval before rework execution. | Maintenance Manager | Medium-low after the control for rework scope added without reapproval passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-128 | Downtime and failure | Downtime interval overlaps incorrectly | two intervals of the same equipment-state class intersect or a correction leaves both old and replacement spans active. | the interval union no longer equals authoritative equipment unavailability and measure totals double-count time. | enforce non-overlap per state class, supersede corrected spans explicitly and recompute the affected certified period. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for downtime interval overlaps incorrectly passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-129 | Downtime and failure | Maintenance downtime treated as production loss | the equipment unavailable interval is copied directly into Manufacturing loss without considering demand, redundancy or WIP. | financial and operational production-loss reporting is overstated. | send Maintenance outage evidence; require Manufacturing to determine demanded capacity and authoritative loss interval. | Manufacturing | Medium-low after the control for maintenance downtime treated as production loss passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-130 | Downtime and failure | Failure mode confused with cause | an observed manner of failure is promoted to verified cause without a causal test. | the selected corrective action may remove the symptom while the initiating mechanism continues. | store the observed mode on the event; open a distinct causal case whose verified state needs disconfirming tests and Reliability sign-off. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for failure mode confused with cause passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-131 | Downtime and failure | Chronic bad actor population incomplete | bad-actor analysis excludes relevant equipment variants, repeat events or periods without documented population rules. | systemic reliability problems remain below escalation thresholds. | version inclusion criteria and compare included events to the authoritative failure population. | Reliability Manager | Low after an independent analyst reproduces the chronic bad actor population incomplete population and result; source-data correction can still require restatement. |
| MR-132 | Downtime and failure | Repair duration excludes waiting inconsistently | some repairs subtract material, permit or vendor waiting while others include it in MTTR. | comparisons across assets and crews are invalid. | publish formula-specific elapsed and active-repair durations from typed intervals rather than editing event times. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for repair duration excludes waiting inconsistently passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-133 | Downtime and failure | Failure code taxonomy changed historically | renaming or reparenting a failure code reclassifies prior events without a taxonomy version. | trend breaks and past reports cannot be reproduced. | version code sets, retain event-time code identity and map restatements explicitly. | Reliability Manager | Low after an independent analyst reproduces the failure code taxonomy changed historically population and result; source-data correction can still require restatement. |
| MR-134 | Downtime and failure | No-fault-found repair closes investigation | a reset or successful test closes the event despite no reproduced fault or cause. | intermittent failure recurs without monitoring or escalation. | retain no-fault-found status, define observation period and trigger repeat-event review. | Reliability Manager | Medium-low after the control for no-fault-found repair closes investigation passes normal, emergency and correction scenarios; field evidence remains reviewable. |
| MR-135 | Downtime and failure | Component failure assigned to parent equipment only | failure evidence stops at the parent asset although a fitted component was identified. | component reliability and warranty populations are incomplete. | capture parent and installed-component identities at event time and validate the installation interval. | Asset Manager | Medium-low after physical-to-system sampling for component failure assigned to parent equipment only; unresolved legacy identity or custody ambiguity may persist. |

### 58.2 Maintenance example catalog

Each example identifies the owning Maintenance role and allowed requests. “No request” means the scenario does not authorize that effect; it does not remove the receiving domain from governance.

| ID | Example | Maintenance owner | Source | Main Maintenance transaction | Maintenance effect | Inventory request | Manufacturing request | Quality request | Procurement request | Safety request | Finance request | Approval | Reconciliation | Specific risk | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ME-001 | Routine calendar PM | Maintenance Planner | Approved plan and due call | Preventive maintenance call | The due call snapshots the active plan, task-list revision, equipment configuration and calendar basis; the planner readies work without changing stock or production. | Routine calendar PM creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | Routine calendar PM does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | Routine calendar PM creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Routine calendar PM creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | Routine calendar PM grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Routine calendar PM posts no financial entry; only accepted labor, material or service quantities can be sent later to Finance for governed accounting. | The Maintenance Manager or delegated supervisor approves the exact revision and exception for routine calendar pm; requester and executor cannot approve their own work. | For routine calendar pm, reconcile plan revision, due-call basis, order completion and next-call calculation; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | A wrong plan or calendar snapshot can call work outside its approved due basis. | Planned |
| ME-002 | Overdue lubrication PM | Maintenance Planner | Approved plan and due call | Preventive maintenance call | The original due date and overdue exposure remain visible while the planner creates a lubrication order and escalates priority against the approved tolerance. | Maintenance requests item/quantity or serial custody for overdue lubrication pm; Inventory owns reservation, movement, location and valuation. | Overdue lubrication PM does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | Overdue lubrication PM creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Overdue lubrication PM creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | Overdue lubrication PM grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Overdue lubrication PM posts no financial entry; only accepted labor, material or service quantities can be sent later to Finance for governed accounting. | The Maintenance Manager or delegated supervisor approves the exact revision and exception for overdue lubrication pm; requester and executor cannot approve their own work. | For overdue lubrication pm, reconcile plan revision, due-call basis, order completion and next-call calculation; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | Resetting the plan can hide overdue lubrication and falsely restore PM compliance. | Planned |
| ME-003 | Approved PM deferral | Maintenance Planner | Approved plan and due call | Preventive maintenance call | Maintenance records a separate deferral decision containing original due date, expiry, risk, compensating inspection and approver; it does not rewrite the PM call. | Approved PM deferral creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | Approved PM deferral does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | Approved PM deferral creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Approved PM deferral creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | Approved PM deferral grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Approved PM deferral posts no financial entry; only accepted labor, material or service quantities can be sent later to Finance for governed accounting. | The Maintenance Manager or delegated supervisor approves the exact revision and exception for approved pm deferral; requester and executor cannot approve their own work. | For approved pm deferral, reconcile plan revision, due-call basis, order completion and next-call calculation; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | Editing the due date can erase the approved deferral, expiry and compensating inspection. | Planned |
| ME-004 | Rejected PM deferral | Maintenance Planner | Approved plan and due call | Preventive maintenance call | The attempted deferral and rejection are retained; the call remains due and returns to scheduling with the rejection rationale and unchanged compliance clock. | Rejected PM deferral creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | Rejected PM deferral does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | Rejected PM deferral creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Rejected PM deferral creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | Rejected PM deferral grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Rejected PM deferral posts no financial entry; only accepted labor, material or service quantities can be sent later to Finance for governed accounting. | The Maintenance Manager or delegated supervisor approves the exact revision and exception for rejected pm deferral; requester and executor cannot approve their own work. | For rejected pm deferral, reconcile plan revision, due-call basis, order completion and next-call calculation; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | A rejected deferral can still shift scheduling if decision status is not enforced. | Planned |

| ME-010 | Suspended PM plan | Maintenance Planner | Approved plan and due call | Preventive maintenance call | Plan suspension stops new calls from its effective time while existing calls await explicit completion, deferral, skip or cancellation disposition. | Suspended PM plan creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | Suspended PM plan does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | Suspended PM plan creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Suspended PM plan creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | Suspended PM plan grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Suspended PM plan posts no financial entry; only accepted labor, material or service quantities can be sent later to Finance for governed accounting. | The Maintenance Manager or delegated supervisor approves the exact revision and exception for suspended pm plan; requester and executor cannot approve their own work. | For suspended pm plan, reconcile plan revision, due-call basis, order completion and next-call calculation; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | Suspension can fail to stop new calls, creating unauthorized preventive demand. | Planned |

| ME-117 | External-service accrual request | Maintenance Manager | Accepted technical quantities | Maintenance closure or classification request | Maintenance confirms service received by order and date; Procurement confirms commercial receipt and Finance decides accrual. | External-service accrual request creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | External-service accrual request does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | External-service accrual request creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Maintenance sends technical scope or acceptance evidence for external-service accrual request; Procurement owns supplier, PO, contract, claim, remedy and commercial receipt. | External-service accrual request grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Maintenance submits accepted technical quantities and references for external-service accrual request; Finance owns rates, account, period, classification, journal and settlement. | Maintenance approves source quantities for external-service accrual request; Finance independently approves classification, posting or settlement as applicable. | For external-service accrual request, reconcile accepted technical quantities, Inventory/Procurement documents, Finance posting and unsettled exceptions; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | An accrual can be recorded for service not technically received or commercially acknowledged. | Planned |
| ME-118 | Cross-plant work assignment | Maintenance Manager | Accepted technical quantities | Maintenance closure or classification request | The receiving plant accepts scoped responsibility and named crew authority while the owning plant retains equipment governance. | Cross-plant work assignment creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | Cross-plant work assignment does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | Cross-plant work assignment creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | Cross-plant work assignment creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | Cross-plant work assignment grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Maintenance submits accepted technical quantities and references for cross-plant work assignment; Finance owns rates, account, period, classification, journal and settlement. | Maintenance approves source quantities for cross-plant work assignment; Finance independently approves classification, posting or settlement as applicable. | For cross-plant work assignment, reconcile accepted technical quantities, Inventory/Procurement documents, Finance posting and unsettled exceptions; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | Cross-plant assignment can bypass owning-plant object scope, competence and release delegation. | Planned |
| ME-119 | AI maintenance recommendation | Maintenance Manager | Accepted technical quantities | Maintenance closure or classification request | The recommendation is stored with model, evidence and confidence for human review and has no approval, issue, release or posting authority. | AI maintenance recommendation creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | AI maintenance recommendation does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | AI maintenance recommendation creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | AI maintenance recommendation creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | AI maintenance recommendation grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Maintenance submits accepted technical quantities and references for ai maintenance recommendation; Finance owns rates, account, period, classification, journal and settlement. | Maintenance approves source quantities for ai maintenance recommendation; Finance independently approves classification, posting or settlement as applicable. | For ai maintenance recommendation, reconcile accepted technical quantities, Inventory/Procurement documents, Finance posting and unsettled exceptions; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | AI advice can acquire implicit approval when users cannot distinguish recommendation from command. | Future |
| ME-120 | AI autonomous release attempt | Maintenance Manager | Accepted technical quantities | Maintenance closure or classification request | The command is denied, equipment state remains unchanged, and Security receives the model, user context, payload and policy decision. | AI autonomous release attempt creates no stock command; any material later required must begin as a separate Maintenance demand acknowledged by Inventory. | AI autonomous release attempt does not change production execution; Manufacturing receives an impact request only if the governed event affects capacity or access. | AI autonomous release attempt creates no Quality disposition; a product or inspection concern must be raised as a separately scoped impact request. | AI autonomous release attempt creates no supplier commitment; Procurement is engaged only through an explicit external-service, warranty or sourcing request. | AI autonomous release attempt grants no permit or isolation; any hazardous operation must obtain a separate scope-matched Safety/HSE acknowledgement. | Maintenance submits accepted technical quantities and references for ai autonomous release attempt; Finance owns rates, account, period, classification, journal and settlement. | No approval is delegated: the attempt is denied; Security investigates and the Asset Manager retains release authority. | For ai autonomous release attempt, reconcile accepted technical quantities, Inventory/Procurement documents, Finance posting and unsettled exceptions; age each missing or mismatched acknowledgement to its owning domain. | An AI release command can bypass human verification and return unsafe equipment to operations. | Future |

### 58.4 Current-versus-target evidence matrix

| Concern | Current repository evidence | Current classification | Target FCSB-020 contract |
|---|---|---|---|
| Organization | Plant, Department, Location, CostCenter and organization-scope services | Implemented foundation | Maintenance plant, planning authority, work center, crew and delegated release scope |
| Technical objects | Manufacturer and SerialNumber masters; ASSET seed registration | Foundation plus registered metadata | Versioned functional location, equipment, installed-component and technical-history aggregates |
| Spares | Item.isMaintenanceSpare, seeded spare group/category/warehouse | Foundation plus registered metadata | Maintenance demand contracts reconciled to Inventory reservation, issue, return and serial custody |
| Work management | Generic transaction kinds/documents, workflow and approvals | Scaffold and registered metadata | Request, notification, order, planning, scheduling, dispatch, execution, verification and release lifecycles |
| Reliability | No dedicated model, service, route or accepted test | Planned | Failure populations, formula versions, MTBF/MTTR, availability, bad actors and health evidence |
| Calibration | No calibration aggregate; Quality architecture defines the boundary | Planned | Metrology-owned instrument state and versioned Quality impact request/acknowledgement |
| Costing | CostCenter and generic finance scaffold | Foundation and scaffold | Maintenance quantities sent to Finance-owned rates, posting, capitalization and settlement |
| Predictive/mobile | No sensor, model, mobile or offline maintenance runtime | Future | Provenance-rich advisory signals and provisional offline evidence with no autonomous authority |

## 59. Architecture Decisions and Open Decisions

Architecture decisions establish non-negotiable ownership and lifecycle separations, while proposed choices remain subject to review. Each rationale names the maintenance consequence and rejects a concrete unsafe alternative such as conflating release with restart or completion with settlement.

Open decisions are not silently answered by this draft. Every item names the evidence required—schemas, authority matrices, state-transition tests, reconciliation cases, formula examples or threat analysis—before the accountable forum can resolve it.

**Controlled concepts:** ADR register; decision status; rationale; unsafe alternative; open decision; evidence gate; accountable forum.

### 59.1 Maintenance architecture decision register

Entries are Proposed unless explicitly Deferred. They require architecture approval and do not authorize implementation.

| ADR | Decision | Status | Maintenance-specific rationale and unsafe alternative rejected |
|---|---|---|---|
| MADR-001 | Maintenance owns equipment condition | Proposed | Equipment condition needs one technical authority and a traceable state history. Reject allowing production, stock or accounting convenience to set equipment condition. |
| MADR-002 | Maintenance owns maintenance hold | Proposed | A Maintenance hold records a technical restriction with explicit scope and criteria. Reject using a Manufacturing stop or Inventory block as an undocumented substitute. |
| MADR-003 | Maintenance owns equipment return-to-service | Proposed | Return-to-service depends on verified repair, tests and open defects. Reject equating technician completion with accountable equipment readiness. |
| MADR-004 | Manufacturing owns production restart | Proposed | Only Manufacturing can assess orders, WIP, labor and production windows before restart. Reject Maintenance changing production execution state after release. |
| MADR-005 | Quality owns product and inspection disposition | Proposed | Inspection fitness and product disposition depend on Quality evidence. Reject Maintenance clearing product or inspection restrictions while repairing equipment. |
| MADR-006 | Inventory owns spare movement | Proposed | Inventory must validate stock, batch, serial, location and valuation for every spare movement. Reject Maintenance decrementing quantity from a work order. |
| MADR-007 | Finance owns maintenance posting | Proposed | Finance applies rates, periods, accounts and settlement policy to accepted maintenance evidence. Reject a breakdown or completion event generating an automatic journal. |
| MADR-008 | Procurement owns supplier commercial action | Proposed | Procurement controls supplier approval, purchase orders, contracts and claims. Reject Maintenance turning technical preference into a commercial commitment. |
| MADR-009 | Safety owns permit and isolation authorization | Proposed | Safety/HSE owns permit and isolation authority where configured. Reject an order status or supervisor note self-authorizing hazardous work. |
| MADR-010 | Work request does not equal work order | Proposed | A request states reported need; an order authorizes governed execution. Reject converting an unassessed symptom directly into released work. |
| MADR-011 | Notification does not equal work order | Proposed | A notification preserves technical assessment and failure evidence before planning. Reject collapsing it into an order and losing diagnostic history. |
| MADR-012 | Work-order completion does not equal equipment release | Proposed | Completion proves tasks were reported; release requires separate verification of equipment condition. Reject a completion button making equipment available. |
| MADR-013 | Equipment release does not equal production restart | Proposed | Maintenance release supplies readiness evidence, while Manufacturing decides operational restart. Reject sending a release command that resumes production automatically. |
| MADR-014 | Spare requirement does not equal reservation | Proposed | A spare requirement is a planning estimate without stock commitment. Reject displaying planned quantity as reserved availability. |
| MADR-015 | Reservation does not equal issue | Proposed | Reservation allocates stock; issue records an authoritative physical movement. Reject treating allocation acknowledgement as consumption or installation. |
| MADR-016 | Maintenance completion does not equal Finance settlement | Proposed | Technical completion leaves rates, accruals, periods and classification unresolved. Reject marking a work order financially settled from Maintenance. |
| MADR-017 | Calibration completion does not equal Quality acceptance of historical results | Proposed | Calibration establishes instrument state; Quality determines effects on inspections and product. Reject a calibration pass/fail rewriting historical inspection acceptance. |
| MADR-018 | Failure code does not equal root cause | Proposed | Failure codes classify observations and hypotheses, not proven causality. Reject closing root-cause analysis from a selected cause code alone. |
| MADR-019 | Asset hierarchy is versioned | Proposed | Versioned hierarchy preserves where equipment and components belonged at each event. Reject overwriting parent links and reinterpreting historical work. |
| MADR-020 | Equipment installation and removal are historical | Proposed | Installation and removal events establish configuration chronology and custody handoff. Reject updating only the current component pointer. |
| MADR-021 | Technical history is immutable | Proposed | Append-only technical history supports audit, reliability and warranty reconstruction. Reject deleting or replacing unfavorable work, readings or failures. |
| MADR-022 | PM plan versions are preserved | Proposed | Every PM call must retain the plan version and calculation inputs used. Reject recalculating prior calls under the newest plan. |
| MADR-023 | PM deferral requires authority | Proposed | Deferral changes exposure and requires scoped authority, expiry and compensating controls. Reject editing the due date to hide overdue work. |
| MADR-024 | Skipped PM is explicit | Proposed | An approved skip remains a visible lifecycle outcome with reason. Reject deleting the call or marking unperformed work complete. |
| MADR-025 | Equipment criticality is versioned | Proposed | Criticality changes influence strategy, priority and spares, so factor lineage must persist. Reject replacing historical class values in place. |
| MADR-026 | Equipment hold scope is explicit | Proposed | Hold scope identifies affected equipment, components and conditions. Reject a plant-wide Boolean that cannot express partial or conditional restriction. |
| MADR-027 | Competing holds are preserved | Proposed | Maintenance, Safety and Quality restrictions can coexist independently. Reject clearing every restriction when one owner releases its hold. |
| MADR-028 | Conditional release is explicit | Proposed | Conditional release must carry operating limits, expiry and recipient acknowledgement. Reject conveying constraints only in technician free text. |
| MADR-029 | Breakdown start and end are evidence-based | Proposed | Evidence-based malfunction timestamps protect downtime and reliability measures. Reject planners backdating intervals to improve performance metrics. |
| MADR-030 | Downtime categories are separate | Proposed | Separate downtime categories explain active repair and waiting causes. Reject one undifferentiated duration that masks material, permit or vendor delay. |
| MADR-031 | Maintenance downtime does not equal production loss | Proposed | Manufacturing determines actual lost production from schedules and capacity. Reject reporting every Maintenance downtime minute as production loss. |
| MADR-032 | Technician time does not equal payroll posting | Proposed | Maintenance confirms technical time while Payroll applies compensation policy. Reject technician confirmation becoming automatic payroll approval. |
| MADR-033 | External service completion does not equal invoice approval | Proposed | Maintenance accepts technical deliverables; Procurement and AP validate commercial and invoice conditions. Reject service completion approving payment. |
| MADR-034 | Warranty eligibility does not equal supplier claim | Proposed | Technical eligibility provides evidence for a Procurement-owned claim. Reject Maintenance creating a supplier receivable from a warranty flag. |
| MADR-035 | Maintenance cannot directly write Inventory | Proposed | Inventory authorization, quantity, serial and valuation controls cannot be bypassed. Reject direct Prisma or API stock updates from Maintenance code. |
| MADR-036 | Maintenance cannot directly restart Manufacturing | Proposed | Production state belongs to Manufacturing even after equipment release. Reject a Maintenance adapter writing restart or operation status. |
| MADR-037 | Maintenance cannot directly release a Quality hold | Proposed | Quality restrictions survive Maintenance repair until Quality decides disposition. Reject equipment release clearing a product hold. |
| MADR-038 | Maintenance cannot directly post Finance | Proposed | Posting and settlement require Finance rules and closed-period controls. Reject Maintenance writing journals, accruals or asset values. |
| MADR-039 | Tool and calibration status remain owning-domain authoritative | Proposed | The domain that owns a tool or calibration state remains authoritative. Reject a copied status on the work order being treated as current truth. |
| MADR-040 | Out-of-tolerance calibration triggers Quality impact assessment | Proposed | Failed calibration creates a suspect measurement interval requiring Quality assessment. Reject Maintenance unilaterally declaring affected inspections valid. |
| MADR-041 | Predictive maintenance output is advisory | Deferred | Predictions carry confidence and model lineage but remain planning advice. Reject a model directly creating approved or dispatched work. |
| MADR-042 | Sensor events are untrusted until validated | Deferred | Sensor identity, time, unit and integrity must be validated before use. Reject raw telemetry changing equipment condition or due dates. |
| MADR-043 | AI cannot approve work orders | Proposed | Order approval is a consequential human or deterministic-policy act. Reject AI elevating its recommendation into approval. |
| MADR-044 | AI cannot release equipment | Proposed | Equipment release requires verified technical evidence and accountable authority. Reject AI changing readiness from a health score. |
| MADR-045 | AI cannot restart production | Proposed | A predictive output lacks WIP status, staffing, material flow and the authorized operating window. Reject exposing any model tool that can invoke the Manufacturing restart command. |
| MADR-046 | AI cannot issue spares | Proposed | Inventory must control allocation and movement of spares. Reject AI converting predicted demand into an issued quantity. |
| MADR-047 | AI cannot approve calibration impact | Proposed | Historical inspection and product populations require Quality judgement over metrology evidence. Reject statistical confidence or a model label closing the impact case. |
| MADR-048 | AI cannot post costs | Proposed | Finance owns rates, accounts, periods and classification. Reject AI turning maintenance estimates into posted cost. |
| MADR-049 | Current asset and equipment-like fields are foundations only | Proposed | Current fields, enums and seed registrations are narrow anchors only. Reject labeling them an equipment, work-order or reliability runtime. |
| MADR-050 | FCSB-020 does not authorize implementation | Proposed | This draft records proposed architecture pending named reviews. Reject beginning schema or service implementation solely because the volume exists. |
| MADR-051 | Functional location is distinct from organization location | Proposed | Organization Location and Functional Location have different purpose and history. Reject reusing organization nodes as installation positions without a governed mapping. |
| MADR-052 | Equipment is distinct from fixed asset | Proposed | Equipment condition and accounting recognition evolve independently. Reject making FixedAsset the Maintenance aggregate or retiring both together automatically. |
| MADR-053 | Equipment is distinct from Inventory serial | Proposed | Inventory serial identifies stock custody; equipment identifies a maintainable installed unit. Reject assuming every serialized item is active equipment. |
| MADR-054 | Machine resource is distinct from equipment | Proposed | Manufacturing resource describes capacity and execution; equipment describes condition and maintenance history. Reject a shared mutable status field. |
| MADR-055 | As-maintained hierarchy is event-derived | Proposed | Installed configuration must be reconstructed from valid installation/removal events. Reject storing only an unversioned current-child collection. |
| MADR-056 | Backdated installation requires overlap validation | Proposed | Backdated changes can create impossible overlapping configurations. Reject accepting them without timeline validation and independent review. |
| MADR-057 | Criticality score retains factor lineage | Proposed | Criticality must retain factor inputs, scale, rationale and version. Reject an unexplained score that cannot support strategy review. |
| MADR-058 | Run-to-failure is an approved strategy | Proposed | Run-to-failure is acceptable only with documented consequence, redundancy and spare assumptions. Reject treating missing PM as an implicit strategy. |
| MADR-059 | PM calls preserve calculation basis | Proposed | A generated PM call must snapshot cycle, counter/calendar input and shift policy. Reject deriving history from the current plan at report time. |
| MADR-060 | Frozen schedule changes are explicit | Proposed | Frozen schedule changes affect commitments and displaced risk. Reject overwriting assignments without reason, approver and displaced-work visibility. |
| MADR-061 | Task-list use snapshots its version | Proposed | Execution must retain the released task-list version and attachments. Reject showing technicians a mutable latest instruction. |
| MADR-062 | Emergency authority permits containment only | Proposed | Emergency authority enables safe containment, not release or financial effects. Reject a broad emergency role that bypasses permits and SoD. |
| MADR-063 | Order revisions trigger proportional reapproval | Proposed | Material scope changes after approval require proportionate revalidation. Reject technicians expanding high-risk work under the original approval. |
| MADR-064 | Dispatch does not create labor evidence | Proposed | Dispatch assigns and requests acknowledgement; it does not prove labor began. Reject creating time from a supervisor's assignment timestamp. |
| MADR-065 | Offline execution is provisional | Proposed | Offline entries remain provisional until identity, order revision and sequence reconcile. Reject last-write-wins synchronization of maintenance evidence. |
| MADR-066 | Technician completion requires independent verification where controlled | Proposed | Controlled work needs verification separate from the performer. Reject a technician self-releasing equipment after recording completion. |
| MADR-067 | Spare substitution requires technical fitness review | Proposed | Substitute spares require technical equivalence and Inventory-controlled issue. Reject warehouse availability alone determining fitness. |
| MADR-068 | Rotable custody reconciles with Inventory | Proposed | Rotable installation/removal must reconcile installed position with off-equipment Inventory custody. Reject independent histories that lose the serialized unit. |
| MADR-069 | Contractor identity is individual and sponsored | Proposed | Contractor actions require a sponsored individual identity and scoped expiry. Reject shared supplier accounts that erase personal attribution. |
| MADR-070 | Maintenance accepts technical service only | Proposed | Maintenance may accept service scope and technical result only. Reject that acceptance changing supplier approval or invoice status. |
| MADR-071 | Equipment release names the installed configuration | Proposed | Release must identify the as-maintained configuration actually verified. Reject releasing a parent asset while component swaps remain unreconciled. |
| MADR-072 | Failure cause remains provisional until verified | Proposed | Cause remains suspected until evidence verifies and alternatives are considered. Reject changing a provisional code into root cause through closure. |
| MADR-073 | Reliability formulas are versioned | Proposed | Reliability measures must preserve population, clocks, exclusions and formula version. Reject a dashboard calculation with hidden mutable assumptions. |
| MADR-074 | Meter corrections append rather than overwrite | Proposed | Meter corrections retain original and corrected readings with reason. Reject overwriting the value used for a prior PM call. |
| MADR-075 | Counter rollover creates a new segment | Proposed | Rollover, reset and replacement create explicit counter segments. Reject subtracting across discontinuities as if the counter were monotonic. |
| MADR-076 | Asset-health score is decomposable | Deferred | Every composite health result must drill into dated observations, normalization, weighting and uncertainty. Reject a black-box rank that operators could confuse with equipment condition or release authority. |
| MADR-077 | Condition alarms create recommendations only | Proposed | Condition alarms can recommend inspection or a request after human review. Reject threshold events directly approving orders or stopping production. |
| MADR-078 | Calibration certificates require provenance | Proposed | Certificates require supplier, standard, method, result and integrity provenance. Reject trusting an attachment merely because its filename says certificate. |
| MADR-079 | Warranty recovery remains Procurement-owned | Proposed | Procurement owns claim submission, negotiation and recovery. Reject Maintenance closing warranty based only on technical eligibility. |
| MADR-080 | Repair-versus-capitalization remains Finance-owned | Proposed | Finance decides expense, capitalization and depreciation effects using Maintenance evidence. Reject technical closure classifying an overhaul as capital. |

## 60. Approval and Roadmap

Approval requires Architecture Board and named domain reviewers to accept ownership, state, evidence, integration, security, safety and reporting contracts. Coding must wait for approved technical-object aggregates, lifecycle guards, effect contracts, reconciliation behavior, retention, SoD and migration strategy.

Version 1.0 Draft is documentation only and does not authorize implementation. FCSB-021 remains Project and Service Management Architecture, with Volumes 22–25 unchanged. Future work must preserve FCSB-020 boundaries and record approved changes through controlled architecture governance.

**Controlled concepts:** approval roles; approval conditions; version history; pre-coding work; cross-domain prerequisites; next volume; documentation-only authority.

~~~mermaid
flowchart TB
    GovernanceAndApproA["Governance and approval"]
    GovernanceAndApproB["documentation-only authority"]
    GovernanceAndApproC["approval roles"]
    GovernanceAndApproD["approval conditions"]
    GovernanceAndApproE["version history"]
    GovernanceAndApproF["pre-coding work"]
    GovernanceAndApproB & GovernanceAndApproC --> GovernanceAndApproD
    GovernanceAndApproD --> GovernanceAndApproE --> GovernanceAndApproF
    GovernanceAndApproF -.-> GovernanceAndApproC
    GovernanceAndApproA -.-> GovernanceAndApproE
~~~

### 60.1 Approval roles and conditions

| Approval role | Approval condition |
|---|---|
| Architecture Board | Accept aggregate boundaries, authority ownership, state separations and the controlled roadmap. |
| Maintenance and Asset Management | Accept equipment identity, hierarchy, condition, work and release governance. |
| Reliability | Accept criticality, failure, formula, health and predictive-advisory definitions. |
| Manufacturing and Production Planning | Accept stop/window/restart contracts without transferring production authority. |
| Inventory and Warehouse | Accept spare/tool request and acknowledgement contracts with Inventory-owned movements. |
| Quality and Metrology | Accept calibration-state and out-of-tolerance impact boundaries. |
| Procurement and Supplier Management | Accept contractor, external-service, warranty and contract commercial boundaries. |
| Finance and Cost Accounting | Accept evidence, posting, capitalization and settlement separations. |
| Engineering and Safety/HSE | Accept configuration references, permit/isolation authority and safe coordination. |
| Security, Data Governance and Integration | Accept scope, SoD, identity, retention, event contracts and threat controls. |
| Reporting, Operations and Internal Audit | Accept measure definitions, support ownership, reconciliation and assurance evidence. |

### 60.2 Work required before Maintenance coding

Before implementation, approve the open decisions; define canonical aggregates and state machines; specify domain request/acknowledgement contracts; prove tenant, plant and object authorization; agree permit and equipment-release controls; define reconciliation and retention; prepare migration/duplicate-resolution rules; and create negative, concurrency, outage, backdated-event and SoD test catalogs. No generic scaffold should be extended into maintenance execution until those gates are traceable to an approved ADR.

### 60.3 Version history

| Version | Date | Status | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.0 Draft | 2026-07-18 | Architecture Review Draft | Initial complete Maintenance and Asset Reliability architecture reference; documentation only. |

### 60.4 Repository evidence reviewed

Evidence review covered README and workspace manifests; FCSB-001 through FCSB-019 and the controlled Series Index; DBA-002, DBA-003 and DBA-004 assessments and implementation reports; Prisma schema, seed and all three accepted migrations; organization, master-data, transaction, workflow, report, layout, customization, enterprise-object, audit, Digital DNA, authentication, common, dashboard, API tests and web settings routes; Docker Compose topology; and Git history/tags through `v0.4-dba004-merged`. Searches found no dedicated asset/equipment/functional-location/maintenance/calibration/reliability controller, service, DTO, route, model or test.

### 60.5 Roadmap boundary

FCSB-020 does not authorize code, schema, migration, seed, API, frontend, test, deployment or operational data changes. FCSB-021 — Project and Service Management Architecture remains the next planned volume; FCSB-022 through FCSB-025 retain their controlled titles and sequence. Any implementation proposal must return through architecture change control with evidence against this volume.
